People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I came in because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m imagining things, but I’m not. My husband keeps saying I’m “getting worked up” and the people at the warehouse keep looking at me like I’m crazy, but I know what’s going on.

They’ve been watching what I do at work, like my moves, where I go, what I scan, what pallets I touch. And the pallet counts keep changing, not by accident. It’s like they’re messing with the numbers so it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing, because I was trying to get that supervisor spot. I know that sounds like a lot, but when you’re there every day and you see the counts one way and then suddenly they’re different, and people are whispering or acting like nothing happened, it’s pretty obvious.

So I’m here because my husband wanted me to come, but also because I’m tired of everyone treating me like I’m making it up.

Yeah… I mean, I don’t like saying it because then people act like that means everything I’m saying is made up, and that’s not what I’m saying. But yes, sometimes I hear something.

It’ll happen at work, like when I’m in one of the aisles by myself and I’m checking labels or counts, and I’ll hear a man’s voice, really clear, like not muffled or like a thought in my head. It’ll say, “Nicole, check it again.” And I’ll turn around because it sounds like somebody is right there, like maybe one of the guys came around the corner or a supervisor was behind me, but nobody’s there. And it’s happened at home too, like when I’m folding clothes, just doing normal stuff, and I hear it again, the same kind of voice telling me to check it again.

I know the difference between thinking something and hearing it. This is more like hearing it with my ears, and it makes me stop what I’m doing. It’s not like I’m asleep or dreaming or anything. I’m awake. I’m right there.

And it’s happened at home too, like when I’m folding clothes, just doing normal stuff, and I hear it again, the same kind of voice telling me to check it again.\n\nI know the difference between thinking something and hearing it. This is more like hearing it with my ears, and it makes me stop what I’m doing. It’s not like I’m asleep or dreaming or anything. I’m awake. I’m right there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others strongly deny, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you or that you have a special connection or mission that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s too many times to be an accident.\n\nI was trying to get that supervisor spot, and I really think they don’t want me to have it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really feel like something is controlling my body, if that’s what you mean, like making me move my arms or anything. But I do feel like what people are doing around me is aimed at me, yes. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain.

At the warehouse, it’s not random. They watch where I go, what I scan, which pallets I touch, even how long I’m standing somewhere. Then the counts change, and somehow it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing. And the thing is, I was trying for that supervisor spot, so it makes sense to me that they wouldn’t want me getting it and would try to make me look bad.

My husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy or making connections that aren’t there, and people at work act like I’m just imagining it, but I’m the one there seeing it happen. It feels like everyone wants me to just ignore it because it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but when you’re living it every day, it’s not strange. It’s obvious they’re watching my moves and changing things around so I don’t get that position.

I mean, I don’t have, like, a printed report where it says “we changed this to mess with Nicole,” if that’s what you mean. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.

But I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.

And I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. But I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel it in how they pay attention to where I go, and then the pallet counts change, and it makes me look like I don’t deserve that supervisor spot. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.

So no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. But in my mind it’s not just a feeling. It’s patterns. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m imagining things, but I’m not. My husband keeps saying I’m “getting worked up” and the people at the warehouse keep looking at me like I’m crazy, but I know what’s going on.\n\nThey’ve been watching what I do at work, like my moves, where I go, what I scan, what pallets I touch. And the pallet counts keep changing, not by accident. It’s like they’re messing with the numbers so it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing, because I was trying to get that supervisor spot. I know that sounds like a lot, but when you’re there every day and you see the counts one way and then suddenly they’re different, and people are whispering or acting like nothing happened, it’s pretty obvious.\n\nSo I’m here because my husband wanted me to come, but also because I’m tired of everyone treating me like I’m making it up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or sensed things that others don’t seem to notice, like voices or other sensations, even when you’re sure you’re awake and not dreaming?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like saying it because then people act like that means everything I’m saying is made up, and that’s not what I’m saying. But yes, sometimes I hear something.\n\nIt’ll happen at work, like when I’m in one of the aisles by myself and I’m checking labels or counts, and I’ll hear a man’s voice, really clear, like not muffled or like a thought in my head. It’ll say, “Nicole, check it again.” And I’ll turn around because it sounds like somebody is right there, like maybe one of the guys came around the corner or a supervisor was behind me, but nobody’s there. And it’s happened at home too, like when I’m folding clothes, just doing normal stuff, and I hear it again, the same kind of voice telling me to check it again.\n\nI know the difference between thinking something and hearing it. This is more like hearing it with my ears, and it makes me stop what I’m doing. It’s not like I’m asleep or dreaming or anything. I’m awake. I’m right there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others strongly deny, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you or that you have a special connection or mission that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually the main thing everyone keeps arguing with me about. My husband keeps saying I’m reading into things or getting myself worked up, and the people at the warehouse act like I’m crazy for noticing it, but I know what I’ve seen.\n\nThey watch what I’m doing. Like, not just normal supervisor-watching, I mean they pay attention to where I go, what pallets I touch, what I scan, how long I’m in an aisle, all of it. And then the pallet counts will be one way when I check them, and later they’re different, and somehow it falls back on me like I don’t know how to do my job. It’s too many times to be an accident.\n\nI was trying to get that supervisor spot, and I really think they don’t want me to have it. So they change things around or make it look like I made mistakes, and then when I say something, everybody acts like I’m being paranoid or dramatic. But I’m there every day. I know my work. I know when something doesn’t add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being controlled by something outside of yourself, or that certain events or actions by others are specifically directed at you, even if they don’t seem to make sense to anyone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like something is controlling my body, if that’s what you mean, like making me move my arms or anything. But I do feel like what people are doing around me is aimed at me, yes. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain.\n\nAt the warehouse, it’s not random. They watch where I go, what I scan, which pallets I touch, even how long I’m standing somewhere. Then the counts change, and somehow it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing. And the thing is, I was trying for that supervisor spot, so it makes sense to me that they wouldn’t want me getting it and would try to make me look bad.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy or making connections that aren’t there, and people at work act like I’m just imagining it, but I’m the one there seeing it happen. It feels like everyone wants me to just ignore it because it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but when you’re living it every day, it’s not strange. It’s obvious they’re watching my moves and changing things around so I don’t get that position.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know people at work are specifically targeting you and changing the pallet counts? Have you ever found any proof or confirmation that this is happening, or is it something you just know in your mind without being able to show it to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don’t have, like, a printed report where it says “we changed this to mess with Nicole,” if that’s what you mean. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.\n\nBut I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.\n\nAnd I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. But I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel it in how they pay attention to where I go, and then the pallet counts change, and it makes me look like I don’t deserve that supervisor spot. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.\n\nSo no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. But in my mind it’s not just a feeling. It’s patterns. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.\n\nBut I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.\n\nAnd I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.\n\nSo no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things people say on TV or in public are specifically directed at you, meant just for you, or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not so much TV, I don’t think. Like, I’m not sitting there watching a commercial thinking it’s talking to me personally or anything like that.

But in public, and especially at work, yes, I do feel like things people do and say are aimed at me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. People will make little comments, or look over when I’m scanning something, or be standing around like they’re not watching, but they are. And then later the pallet counts are different, or someone brings up a mistake like it just magically happened, and it all circles back to me.

My husband keeps saying I’m taking things wrong or making it about me, but I came in because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it. I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’ve been changing the pallet counts, or doing something with them, to make me look bad so I don’t get that supervisor spot.

So if you mean, do I think every random thing in the world is a message for me, no. But at work? Yes. Those things are meant for me. They’re not just random.

They watch where I go, what I scan, which pallets I touch, even how long I’m standing somewhere. Then the counts change, and somehow it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing. And the thing is, I was trying for that supervisor spot, so it makes sense to me that they wouldn’t want me getting it and would try to make me look bad.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy or making connections that aren’t there, and people at work act like I’m just imagining it, but I’m the one there seeing it happen. It feels like everyone wants me to just ignore it because it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but when you’re living it every day, it’s not strange. It’s obvious they’re watching my moves and changing things around so I don’t get that position.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know people at work are specifically targeting you and changing the pallet counts? Have you ever found any proof or confirmation that this is happening, or is it something you just know in your mind without being able to show it to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don’t have, like, a printed report where it says “we changed this to mess with Nicole,” if that’s what you mean. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.\n\nBut I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.\n\nAnd I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. But I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel it in how they pay attention to where I go, and then the pallet counts change, and it makes me look like I don’t deserve that supervisor spot. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.\n\nSo no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. But in my mind it’s not just a feeling. It’s patterns. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things people say on TV or in public are specifically directed at you, meant just for you, or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV, I don’t think. Like, I’m not sitting there watching a commercial thinking it’s talking to me personally or anything like that.\n\nBut in public, and especially at work, yes, I do feel like things people do and say are aimed at me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. People will make little comments, or look over when I’m scanning something, or be standing around like they’re not watching, but they are. And then later the pallet counts are different, or someone brings up a mistake like it just magically happened, and it all circles back to me.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m taking things wrong or making it about me, but I came in because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it. I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’ve been changing the pallet counts, or doing something with them, to make me look bad so I don’t get that supervisor spot.\n\nSo if you mean, do I think every random thing in the world is a message for me, no. But at work? Yes. Those things are meant for me. Outside of work… not really in the same way. I mean, my husband is part of it because he hears me talk about it and then he says I’m acting crazy, like I’m making all this up or seeing things wrong. It’s mainly the warehouse. That’s where it all connects. And I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, because everybody keeps making that face like, “Here she goes again,” but that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.

I came in because my husband and the people at work keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know they’ve been watching my moves and messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that people keep skipping over. It isn’t just random people in public or the TV or something. It’s specific. It’s happening there, around that job, around that position I was trying to get.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d call it a “special mission,” like I’m not saying I was chosen by God or something like that. It’s not like that. But I do feel like there’s a role I’m supposed to have at work, if that’s what you mean. Like I’ve been trying to move up, trying to get that supervisor spot, and I know I could do it. I know the job, I know the counts, I know how things are supposed to run.

And that’s why this has been so frustrating, because people keep acting like it’s all in my head. My husband says I’m making too much of it, and the warehouse people look at me like I’m crazy when I say something, but I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’re paying attention to what pallets I touch and then changing the counts or messing with them so it comes back on me. It makes me look unreliable, like I can’t handle more responsibility, and that’s exactly what would keep me from getting that position.

So no, I don’t think I have some secret mission nobody can know about. But I do think I’m supposed to be taken seriously there, and instead they’re trying to block me from that supervisor role. That’s the part nobody wants to hear.

It feels like everyone wants me to just ignore it because it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but when you’re living it every day, it’s not strange. It’s obvious they’re watching my moves and changing things around so I don’t get that position.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know people at work are specifically targeting you and changing the pallet counts? Have you ever found any proof or confirmation that this is happening, or is it something you just know in your mind without being able to show it to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don’t have, like, a printed report where it says “we changed this to mess with Nicole,” if that’s what you mean. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.\n\nBut I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.\n\nAnd I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. But I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel it in how they pay attention to where I go, and then the pallet counts change, and it makes me look like I don’t deserve that supervisor spot. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.\n\nSo no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. But in my mind it’s not just a feeling. It’s patterns. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things people say on TV or in public are specifically directed at you, meant just for you, or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV, I don’t think. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. People will make little comments, or look over when I’m scanning something, or be standing around like they’re not watching, but they are. And then later the pallet counts are different, or someone brings up a mistake like it just magically happened, and it all circles back to me.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m taking things wrong or making it about me, but I came in because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it. So at home it comes up because he keeps telling me I need to let it go, but I can’t just let it go when I know what’s happening.\n\nIt’s mainly the warehouse. That’s where it all connects. That’s where they’re watching what I’m doing, where I’m scanning, what pallets I’m touching, how long I’m in certain spots, and then the counts are different or something gets brought back on me like I messed it up. And I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, because everybody keeps making that face like, “Here she goes again,” but that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nI came in because my husband and the people at work keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know they’ve been watching my moves and messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that people keep skipping over. It isn’t just random people in public or the TV or something. It’s specific. It’s happening there, around that job, around that position I was trying to get.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have a special mission, purpose, or role that other people don’t understand or aren’t meant to know about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “special mission,” like I’m not saying I was chosen by God or something like that. It’s not like that. But I do feel like there’s a role I’m supposed to have at work, if that’s what you mean. Like I’ve been trying to move up, trying to get that supervisor spot, and I know I could do it. I know the job, I know the counts, I know how things are supposed to run.\n\nAnd that’s why this has been so frustrating, because people keep acting like it’s all in my head. My husband says I’m making too much of it, and the warehouse people look at me like I’m crazy when I say something, but I know they’ve been watching my moves. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things people say on TV or in public are specifically directed at you, meant just for you, or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV, I don’t think. And then later the pallet counts are different, or someone brings up a mistake like it just magically happened, and it all circles back to me.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m taking things wrong or making it about me, but I came in because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it. So at home it comes up because he keeps telling me I need to let it go, but I can’t just let it go when I know what’s happening.\n\nIt’s mainly the warehouse. That’s where it all connects. That’s where they’re watching what I’m doing, where I’m scanning, what pallets I’m touching, how long I’m in certain spots, and then the counts are different or something gets brought back on me like I messed it up. And I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, because everybody keeps making that face like, “Here she goes again,” but that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nI came in because my husband and the people at work keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know they’ve been watching my moves and messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that people keep skipping over. It isn’t just random people in public or the TV or something. It’s specific. It’s happening there, around that job, around that position I was trying to get.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have a special mission, purpose, or role that other people don’t understand or aren’t meant to know about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “special mission,” like I’m not saying I was chosen by God or something like that. It’s not like that. But I do feel like there’s a role I’m supposed to have at work, if that’s what you mean. Like I’ve been trying to move up, trying to get that supervisor spot, and I know I could do it. I know the job, I know the counts, I know how things are supposed to run.\n\nAnd that’s why this has been so frustrating, because people keep acting like it’s all in my head. My husband says I’m making too much of it, and the warehouse people look at me like I’m crazy when I say something, but I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’re paying attention to what pallets I touch and then changing the counts or messing with them so it comes back on me. It makes me look unreliable, like I can’t handle more responsibility, and that’s exactly what would keep me from getting that position.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I have some secret mission nobody can know about. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really outside of work, no. I mean, it follows me home in the sense that my husband keeps telling me I’m acting crazy or reading into things, and then we argue about it, so it feels like everyone is trying to make me doubt myself. But if you mean like strangers at the store saying something and I think it’s a message for me, or TV shows having hidden meanings for me, no, that’s not really it.

What I’m talking about is the warehouse. That’s where it’s happening. That’s where people are watching my moves, paying attention to where I go, what pallets I touch, what I scan, and then somehow the counts change and it gets put back on me like I’m the one messing up. And I came in because my husband and the people there keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know what I’ve seen. I know they’re messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that matters.

No… I don’t think so. I mean, not like something is controlling my hands or putting thoughts in my head or making me do things when I’m not choosing to do them. That’s not really what I’ve been trying to explain.

So… no, I haven’t really felt controlled like that. I just feel like people don’t believe me about what’s happening at work.

But at work? Yes. Those things are meant for me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… yes, but mostly at work. That’s where it feels connected to me, like people act like it’s just random warehouse stuff or mistakes in the system, but it doesn’t feel random when it keeps happening around the pallets I touched or the counts I was responsible for.

Like I’ll move something, scan something, do what I’m supposed to do, and then later the count is off or somebody is looking at me a certain way, and then it comes back like I’m the one who messed it up. And maybe someone else would say, “Oh, that’s just warehouse errors,” but when it keeps lining up with me trying to move up and get that supervisor spot, it feels like there’s a reason. Like they’re watching my moves and then changing things so it makes me look bad.

That’s why I came in, honestly. My husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy, and people at work look at me like I’m crazy when I bring it up, but I know what I’ve seen. I’m not saying every little thing in the whole world is about me, but at the warehouse, those actions are connected to me. They’re doing it to keep me from getting that position.

It’s like they’re messing with the numbers so it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing, because I was trying to get that supervisor spot. I know that sounds like a lot, but when you’re there every day and you see the counts one way and then suddenly they’re different, and people are whispering or acting like nothing happened, it’s pretty obvious.\n\nSo I’m here because my husband wanted me to come, but also because I’m tired of everyone treating me like I’m making it up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or sensed things that others don’t seem to notice, like voices or other sensations, even when you’re sure you’re awake and not dreaming?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like saying it because then people act like that means everything I’m saying is made up, and that’s not what I’m saying. But yes, sometimes I hear something.\n\nIt’ll happen at work, like when I’m in one of the aisles by myself and I’m checking labels or counts, and I’ll hear a man’s voice, really clear, like not muffled or like a thought in my head. It’ll say, “Nicole, check it again.” And I’ll turn around because it sounds like somebody is right there, like maybe one of the guys came around the corner or a supervisor was behind me, but nobody’s there. My husband keeps saying I’m reading into things or getting myself worked up, and the people at the warehouse act like I’m crazy for noticing it, but I know what I’ve seen.\n\nThey watch what I’m doing. Like, not just normal supervisor-watching, I mean they pay attention to where I go, what pallets I touch, what I scan, how long I’m in an aisle, all of it. And then the pallet counts will be one way when I check them, and later they’re different, and somehow it falls back on me like I don’t know how to do my job. It’s too many times to be an accident.\n\nI was trying to get that supervisor spot, and I really think they don’t want me to have it. I know when something doesn’t add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being controlled by something outside of yourself, or that certain events or actions by others are specifically directed at you, even if they don’t seem to make sense to anyone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like something is controlling my body, if that’s what you mean, like making me move my arms or anything. But I do feel like what people are doing around me is aimed at me, yes. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain.\n\nAt the warehouse, it’s not random. They watch where I go, what I scan, which pallets I touch, even how long I’m standing somewhere. Then the counts change, and somehow it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing. And the thing is, I was trying for that supervisor spot, so it makes sense to me that they wouldn’t want me getting it and would try to make me look bad.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy or making connections that aren’t there, and people at work act like I’m just imagining it, but I’m the one there seeing it happen. It feels like everyone wants me to just ignore it because it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but when you’re living it every day, it’s not strange. Have you ever found any proof or confirmation that this is happening, or is it something you just know in your mind without being able to show it to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don’t have, like, a printed report where it says “we changed this to mess with Nicole,” if that’s what you mean. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.\n\nBut I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.\n\nAnd I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. But I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel it in how they pay attention to where I go, and then the pallet counts change, and it makes me look like I don’t deserve that supervisor spot. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.\n\nSo no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. But in my mind it’s not just a feeling. It’s patterns. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things people say on TV or in public are specifically directed at you, meant just for you, or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV, I don’t think. Like, I’m not sitting there watching a commercial thinking it’s talking to me personally or anything like that.\n\nBut in public, and especially at work, yes, I do feel like things people do and say are aimed at me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. People will make little comments, or look over when I’m scanning something, or be standing around like they’re not watching, but they are. And then later the pallet counts are different, or someone brings up a mistake like it just magically happened, and it all circles back to me.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m taking things wrong or making it about me, but I came in because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it. I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’ve been changing the pallet counts, or doing something with them, to make me look bad so I don’t get that supervisor spot.\n\nSo if you mean, do I think every random thing in the world is a message for me, no. But at work? Yes. Those things are meant for me. They’re not just random.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that things people do or say, or events that happen, are specifically about you or meant for you, even if they don’t seem connected to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really in the same way. I mean, my husband is part of it because he hears me talk about it and then he says I’m acting crazy, like I’m making all this up or seeing things wrong. So at home it comes up because he keeps telling me I need to let it go, but I can’t just let it go when I know what’s happening.\n\nIt’s mainly the warehouse. That’s where it all connects. That’s where they’re watching what I’m doing, where I’m scanning, what pallets I’m touching, how long I’m in certain spots, and then the counts are different or something gets brought back on me like I messed it up. And I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, because everybody keeps making that face like, “Here she goes again,” but that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nI came in because my husband and the people at work keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know they’ve been watching my moves and messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that people keep skipping over. It isn’t just random people in public or the TV or something. It’s specific. It’s happening there, around that job, around that position I was trying to get.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have a special mission, purpose, or role that other people don’t understand or aren’t meant to know about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “special mission,” like I’m not saying I was chosen by God or something like that. Yes. That’s exactly what I’ve been trying to tell people. It’s been going on long enough that it’s not just one bad day or one mistake in the system. It’s too many times to be an accident.\n\nI was trying to get that supervisor spot, and I really think they don’t want me to have it. So they change things around or make it look like I made mistakes, and then when I say something, everybody acts like I’m being paranoid or dramatic. But I’m there every day. I know my work. I know when something doesn’t add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being controlled by something outside of yourself, or that certain events or actions by others are specifically directed at you, even if they don’t seem to make sense to anyone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t really feel like something is controlling my body, if that’s what you mean, like making me move my arms or anything. But I do feel like what people are doing around me is aimed at me, yes. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain.\n\nAt the warehouse, it’s not random. They watch where I go, what I scan, which pallets I touch, even how long I’m standing somewhere. Then the counts change, and somehow it makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing. And the thing is, I was trying for that supervisor spot, so it makes sense to me that they wouldn’t want me getting it and would try to make me look bad.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy or making connections that aren’t there, and people at work act like I’m just imagining it, but I’m the one there seeing it happen. It feels like everyone wants me to just ignore it because it sounds strange when I say it out loud, but when you’re living it every day, it’s not strange. It’s obvious they’re watching my moves and changing things around so I don’t get that position.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know people at work are specifically targeting you and changing the pallet counts? Have you ever found any proof or confirmation that this is happening, or is it something you just know in your mind without being able to show it to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don’t have, like, a printed report where it says “we changed this to mess with Nicole,” if that’s what you mean. That’s the problem, is everybody keeps asking me for proof like that, and of course they’re not going to leave it out in the open. They’re not stupid.\n\nBut I know what I’m seeing. I come in, I do my counts, I know what I scanned, I know what pallets I touched, and then later it’s different, or someone acts like I did something wrong. And it’s always around the same stuff, always when it could make me look bad. It’s not just one little mistake. If it was one time, fine, maybe I’d say I was tired or I counted wrong, but it keeps happening.\n\nAnd I came in here because my husband and people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it out loud. But I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel it in how they pay attention to where I go, and then the pallet counts change, and it makes me look like I don’t deserve that supervisor spot. That’s what this is about. I’ve been trying to move up, and suddenly all this stuff starts happening where I look unreliable.\n\nSo no, I don’t have some big proof I can hand you. But in my mind it’s not just a feeling. It’s patterns. It’s what happens over and over, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things people say on TV or in public are specifically directed at you, meant just for you, or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV, I don’t think. Like, I’m not sitting there watching a commercial thinking it’s talking to me personally or anything like that.\n\nBut in public, and especially at work, yes, I do feel like things people do and say are aimed at me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. People will make little comments, or look over when I’m scanning something, or be standing around like they’re not watching, but they are. And then later the pallet counts are different, or someone brings up a mistake like it just magically happened, and it all circles back to me.\n\nMy husband keeps saying I’m taking things wrong or making it about me, but I came in because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it. I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’ve been changing the pallet counts, or doing something with them, to make me look bad so I don’t get that supervisor spot.\n\nSo if you mean, do I think every random thing in the world is a message for me, no. But at work? Yes. Those things are meant for me. They’re not just random.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that things people do or say, or events that happen, are specifically about you or meant for you, even if they don’t seem connected to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really in the same way. I mean, my husband is part of it because he hears me talk about it and then he says I’m acting crazy, like I’m making all this up or seeing things wrong. So at home it comes up because he keeps telling me I need to let it go, but I can’t just let it go when I know what’s happening.\n\nIt’s mainly the warehouse. That’s where it all connects. That’s where they’re watching what I’m doing, where I’m scanning, what pallets I’m touching, how long I’m in certain spots, and then the counts are different or something gets brought back on me like I messed it up. And I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, because everybody keeps making that face like, “Here she goes again,” but that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nI came in because my husband and the people at work keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know they’ve been watching my moves and messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that people keep skipping over. It isn’t just random people in public or the TV or something. It’s specific. It’s happening there, around that job, around that position I was trying to get.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have a special mission, purpose, or role that other people don’t understand or aren’t meant to know about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “special mission,” like I’m not saying I was chosen by God or something like that. It’s not like that. But I do feel like there’s a role I’m supposed to have at work, if that’s what you mean. Like I’ve been trying to move up, trying to get that supervisor spot, and I know I could do it. I know the job, I know the counts, I know how things are supposed to run.\n\nAnd that’s why this has been so frustrating, because people keep acting like it’s all in my head. My husband says I’m making too much of it, and the warehouse people look at me like I’m crazy when I say something, but I know they’ve been watching my moves. I know they’re paying attention to what pallets I touch and then changing the counts or messing with them so it comes back on me. It makes me look unreliable, like I can’t handle more responsibility, and that’s exactly what would keep me from getting that position.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I have some secret mission nobody can know about. But I do think I’m supposed to be taken seriously there, and instead they’re trying to block me from that supervisor role. That’s the part nobody wants to hear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do or say in other parts of your life — not at work — are meant for you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean, it follows me home in the sense that my husband keeps telling me I’m acting crazy or reading into things, and then we argue about it, so it feels like everyone is trying to make me doubt myself. But if you mean like strangers at the store saying something and I think it’s a message for me, or TV shows having hidden meanings for me, no, that’s not really it.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is the warehouse. That’s where it’s happening. That’s where people are watching my moves, paying attention to where I go, what pallets I touch, what I scan, and then somehow the counts change and it gets put back on me like I’m the one messing up. And I came in because my husband and the people there keep acting like I’m crazy for saying it, but I know what I’ve seen. I know they’re messing with those pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. That’s the part that matters.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions or thoughts were being influenced or controlled by something outside of yourself, even when you weren't at work?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, not like something is controlling my hands or putting thoughts in my head or making me do things when I’m not choosing to do them. That’s not really what I’ve been trying to explain.\n\nWhen I’m not at work, I still think about it a lot, because it bothers me and I go over it in my head, like, “How did that count change?” or “Why did they look at me like that?” But that feels like me thinking about it, not something outside controlling me. And at home, yeah, my husband will say I’m letting it get to me or that I need to stop talking about it, but that’s him saying that, not some outside force controlling my thoughts.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really felt controlled like that. I just feel like people don’t believe me about what’s happening at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like people's actions or events in general, have a hidden meaning or are connected to you in some way, even when they don't seem related to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, but mostly at work. That’s where it feels connected to me, like people act like it’s just random warehouse stuff or mistakes in the system, but it doesn’t feel random when it keeps happening around the pallets I touched or the counts I was responsible for.\n\nLike I’ll move something, scan something, do what I’m supposed to do, and then later the count is off or somebody is looking at me a certain way, and then it comes back like I’m the one who messed it up. And maybe someone else would say, “Oh, that’s just warehouse errors,” but when it keeps lining up with me trying to move up and get that supervisor spot, it feels like there’s a reason. Like they’re watching my moves and then changing things so it makes me look bad.\n\nThat’s why I came in, honestly. My husband keeps saying I’m acting crazy, and people at work look at me like I’m crazy when I bring it up, but I know what I’ve seen. I’m not saying every little thing in the whole world is about me, but at the warehouse, those actions are connected to me. They’re doing it to keep me from getting that position.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt, for at least a month, that you were being followed, watched, or that people were conspiring against you at work, even when others told you there was no evidence for it?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s exactly what I’ve been trying to tell people. It’s been going on long enough that it’s not just one bad day or one mistake in the system. For more than a month, I’ve felt like they’re watching what I do at the warehouse — like which pallets I touch, where I move them, what I scan — and then somehow the numbers get changed and it comes back on me.\n\nAnd everybody keeps acting like I’m making it up. My husband says I’m acting crazy, and people at work look at me like I’m being paranoid or causing drama, but I know what I’ve seen. It’s too lined up with me trying to get that supervisor spot. The counts don’t just randomly go wrong around my work over and over like that.\n\nSo yes, I do feel like they’re watching my moves and messing with the pallet counts to make me look bad, like they’re trying to keep me from getting promoted. That’s why I came in, because people keep telling me there’s no proof, but I know something is happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think it’s like I have some special power or something, if that’s what you mean. I’m not saying I’m better than everybody or that I have some gift they don’t see.

What I’m saying is more that I’m being treated unfairly at work. They don’t say it openly, obviously, but that’s what it feels like. Not special importance, really… just that they’ve got their eyes on me and they’re messing with my work."
